Let's design local optimization more concretely.

Surrogate score with normalized p (sum=1) and full convolution via FFT:
```
def conv_full_fft(a, b=None):
    if b is None: b=a
    n=len(a); m=len(b)
    L=1
    while L < n+m-1: L <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L); fb=np.fft.rfft(b,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb, L)
    return c[:n+m-1]
```
For score max: `M = conv.max(); S=a.sum(); return 2*len(a)*M/S*S`.
Since p sum1, return 2n max.
Soft max: `tau*logsumexp(b/tau)`. But if b values ~1/n, for n=1024, b max ~0.0004. Need τ comparable. We can set τ = 0.05 * max_val or n? For hard max, τ small. Use softplus stable.
For gradient, compute q=exp((b-bmax)/τ)/sum. Then g = convolve_direction? For each i, ∂b_k/∂p_i = sum over k q_k p_{k-i}. That's convolution of q and p? Specifically g_i = ∑_k q_k p_{k-i} where p_j=0 outside 0..n-1. This is (p * q_full?) Let q length 2n-1. Compute h=convolve(p, q, full?) We need for i: ∑_{k} q_k p_{k-i}. Let r = q reversed? If define q_rev[k]=q_{L-1-k}? Then h_i = ∑_k q_k p_{k-i} = (p * q)_? We can just compute via FFT: P=fft(p,L); Q=fft(q,L); then c=ifft(P*Q). For full convolution of p (0..n-1) and q (0..2n-2), coefficient c_m = ∑_{i+k=m} p_i q_k. We need index? For i: set m = ? c? ∑_k q_k p_{k-i}. Let j=k-i => k=j+i. Sum q_{j+i}p_j. That's cross-correlation, not convolution. If we reverse q: R_t=q_{L-1-t}. Then convolution p*R: (p*R)_m=∑_j p_j R_{m-j}=∑_j p_j q_{L-1-(m-j)} not simple. - `soft_score_p(p, tau)`: p sum1. b=conv; M=max; Msoft = tau*logsumexp((b-M)/tau)+M; return 2*n*Msoft.
- `grad_soft_p(p, tau, q=None, b=None)`: compute q from b if not; g via cross-correlation. Need implement efficiently.
```
def conv_full_fft(a, b=None):
    if b is None: b=a
    na=a.size; nb=b.size
    L=1
    L<na+nb-1: L<<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    if b is a: fb=fa
    else: fb=np.fft.rfft(b,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,L)
    return c[:na+nb-1]
```
For gradient:
Input p length n, q length 2n-1 (nonneg sum not necessarily 1? For Msoft q sum1). Need g_i =∑_k q_k p_{k-i} with p outside 0. As derived:
- q_rev = np.empty(L); q_rev[:2n-1] = q[::-1]; L≥3n-2.
But we can compute cross-correlation directly with FFT: `g = ifft(fft(p, L) * conj(fft(q, L)))` maybe gives one of lags. Let's verify sign with simple small.
Let p=[p0,p1], q length3 [q0,q1,q2]. Desired g0=q0p0+q1p1, g1=q1p0+q2p1.
Compute circular correlation `ifft(P * conj(Q))`. For L≥? linear. General formula: at lag d, c[d] = ∑_m p[m] q[m-d] (with Q indices). d=0: p0q0+p1q1. d=1: p0q_{-1}+p1q0 = p1q0. d=-1 (L-1): p0q1? Let's map. Desired g0 = p0q0 + p1q1? Wait for i=0: ∑_k q_k p_{k}. k=0 p0, k=1 p1, k=2 p2 out=0 => p0q0+p1q1. This is correlation lag 0. Desired g1 for i=1: ∑_k q_k p_{k-1}: k=1 p0 q1, k=2 p1 q2 => p0q1+p1q2. This is? correlation lag? c[?]=∑ p[m] q[m-d]. For d=-1: m=0 q1? q[1], m=1 q[2]? q[2] -> p0q1+p1q2. So desired g_i = correlation lag -i. Thus if we compute `rate = ifft(P * conj(Q))` length L, `rate[L-1:-i?` Specifically lag -i corresponds index L-i? Since circular lag d where q[m-d]. d=-i => q[m+i]. For i=1 index L-1. For i=0 index0. So g_i = rate[(L - i) % L]? For i=0 index0; i=1 L-1; i=2 L-2. Thus `g = rate[0:n]` reversed? indices [0, L-1, L-2, ..., L-n+1]. For L large enough >2n, no wrap. So:
```
L=1; while L<3*n-2: L<<=1
P=np.fft.rfft(p,L)
Q=np.fft.rfft(q,L)

```

def build_convolution_matrix(n):

    cols = np.tile(np.arange(n), n)

LP can be run as a hill-climb:
```
def lp_local_search(f, iters, time_limit):
  f=normalize(f)
  for _ in range(iters):
    M=max(conv(f,f))
    g=solve_lp(f,M)
    if g is None: break
    sg=g.sum(); if sg<=0: break
    g/=sg
    best_new=f copy
    for t in [0.1,0.2,0.5,0.8,1.0]:
       cand=(1-t)f+t*g; score=hard
       if cand: update
    if no improvement or best_new is f: maybe do small random? break
    f=best_new
  return f
```

Function `fourier_equalize(p, iters, lam, log_clip, mix, check_interval)`:
- Use time? maybe return best after fixed iters.
- Ensure p positive: p = np.maximum(p, 0); p += 1e-300; normalize.

- `L = 1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()`? Python: `L=1; while L<2*n-1: L*=2`.
- `q_flat = np.zeros(L); q_flat[:2*n-1] = 1.0/(2*n-1)`.
- For iter in range(iters):
   if time? We'll manage outside with max time.
   `P = np.fft.rfft(p, L)` (complex)

Algorithm for target clip:
```
def make_clip_target(B, q_flat, peak_factor, flat_mix):
    B = B/B.sum()
    T = B.max()*peak_factor
    if not finite or T<=0: return flat
    high = B > T
    excess = np.sum(B[high]-T)
    B[high] = T
    low = ~high
    if low.sum()>0:
        B[low] += excess / low.sum()
    Q = (1-flat_mix)*B + flat_mix*q_flat
    Q/=Q.sum(); return Q
```
If all high? impossible. Use.

```
def subgradient_polish(p, iters=1000, eps_schedule=None, eta0=0.2):
   p = normalize
   best = p, score
   eta = eta0
   eps = 0.05
   improve_count=0
   for it in range(iters):
      b=conv_full(p,p); m=b.max(); active = b >= m*(1-eps)
      q=abs? q=active/active.sum()
      g=grad_conv(p,q)
      # line search etas
      best_new=p; best_sc=score
      for cand_eta in [eta, eta*0.5, eta*0.2, eta*0.05]:
         z = -cand_eta * g / (p + 1e-300)
         # to avoid overflow, clip z: z = np.clip(z, -log_clip_update, log_clip_update)
         z -= z.max()
         p2=np.exp(z); s=p2.sum(); if s>0: p2/=s
         sc=hard_score_fft(p2)
         if sc<best_sc: update
      if improved:
         p=best_new; score=best_sc; best; eta=min(eta*1.1,2); maybe decrease eps slowly
      else:
         eta*=0.5; if eta<1e-4: eta=0.05; # reduce eps? maybe keep
         if many no improve: break
      # occasionally exact? if time.
   return best
```

LP implementation detailed:
```
def convolution_matrix(n):
    total = n*n
    rows = np.empty(total, dtype=np.int64)
    cols = np.empty(total, dtype=np.int64)
    # vectorized:
    c = np.arange(n)
    r0 = np.repeat(c, n)
    offsets = np.tile(np.arange(n), n)
    rows = r0 + offsets
    cols = np.tile(c, n)
    return rows, cols
```
Rows max j+n-1 <=2n-2. Good. Data `np.tile(f,n)` each call.
`lp_next(f, M, timeout=20)`:

- `data = np.tile(f, n)`.
- `A = sparse.csr_matrix((data,(rows,cols)), shape=(2*n-1,n))` (note rows, cols length total). For csr, indexes must be C-order? okay.
- `c = -np.ones(n)`
- `b = np.full(2*n-1, M)`
- `bounds = [(0, None)]*n`
- `res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'time_limit': timeout, 'output_flag': False})`
- if res.status==0 or 3? (3 acceptable? timeouts maybe status 3 unacceptable?). `g = res.x` if finite and g>=-1e-9. Check conv(f,g) ≤ M*(1+1e-4)+1e-8? Use our `fft_convolve(f,g)`? - g = np.maximum(g,0); sum; if sum>0 normalize.
Note: HiGHS status codes: 0 optimal, 1 infeasible, 2 unbounded, 3 iteration limit, 4 numerical, 5? If status 3, x may satisfy? Accept if feasible. We'll accept if objective finite and maxviol small.

Take distribution p as mixture of two independent components: e.g., p = 1/2 Dirac block? If we choose p_i nonzero only at positions that form a modular Sidon in gcd blocks, objective? Let length n = M*K. Let p_i = q_{i mod M} * r_{i div M}? Kronecker product. The 1D convolution of Kronecker `p_{a + M b} = q_a r_b`. Its full convolution coefficient at `c + M d` receives carries from lower digit sums. It equals sum over s (q*q)_{a?} (r*r) with carry. Specifically (a,b) base M: sum over b1+b2=d or d-1 depending low sum >=M. Coeff = A_{low} B_{d} + A_{low} B_{d-1} etc, where A length 2M, B length2K. Maximum roughly max of combinations. Objective =2n max /(S^2) with n=MK. If low sequence A has max α S_A^2, base M? Let's derive continuous 2D: If no carry and mapping compact? If M large >? The coefficient max ≤ max(A)max(B) maybe times. Sum S=S_A S_B. n=MK (support within M*K). Objective =2MK * maxAA*maxBB / S_A^2 S_B^2. If A (lower) and B (upper) individually have objective R_A=2M maxAA/S_A^2? maxAA = R_A S_A^2/(2M). B: maxBB=R_B S_B^2/(2K). Product maxAA maxBB = R_A R_B S_A^2 S_B^2/(4MK). Multiply 2MK gives R_A R_B /2. If no carry. But n for full support MK, coefficient bins 2MK-1. This suggests tensor product can reduce objective multiplicatively by 1/2 below product? For A uniform R_A=2, B uniform R_B=2 -> R=2, okay. If A has R_A=1.5, B=1.5 -> R=1.125? But is it feasible? This is promising! But carry and nonzero support complicates. Maybe we can recursively construct lower objective via 2D product if choose length N = m*K and 1D index i + m j (Kronecker). Then the 1D convolution coefficient at index c + m d includes contributions from lower digit sum s = c mod m and carry to d. The max might be close to max over combinations. Let's analyze exact for Kronecker p_{i+m j}=a_i b_j, lengths m,K. Total length N=mK. Sum=SaSb. Convolution: coefficient at k. Write k = u + m v, with 0≤u<m. The pairs (i1,j1),(i2,j2) such that i1+i2 + m(j1+j2)=u + m v. Let s=i1+i2. Then v = j1+j2 + floor(s/m) if s lateral? Actually s may be up to2m-2. Write s = u + m t where u=s mod m, t=floor(s/m). Then need j1+j2 = v - t. So coefficient C_{u,v} = ∑_{s ≡ u mod m} A_s B_{v - t(s)} where t(s)=floor(s/m) =0 if s<m, 1 if s≥m. Thus C = ∑_{s' = u or u+m} A_{s'} B_{v - floor(s'/m)} = A_u B_v + A_{u+m} B_{v-1} (with valid indices). So each coefficient is sum of at most two products of coefficients of A and B, one without carry, one with carry. Max ≤ max_s A_s * max_v B_v + ... Actually C_{u,v} ≤ A_u B_v + A_{u+m} B_{v-1}. Let α_s = max over A_s, α_{u+m}; β max. Max C ≤ α_A α_B + α_A α_B = 2 α_A α_B, because sum of two products. If α_A = R_A Sa^2/(2m), α_B=R_B Sb^2/(2K), then 2 α_A α_B = R_A R_B Sa^2 Sb^2/(4mK). Objective =2mK * that /Sa^2Sb^2 = R_A R_B /2. Same 1/2 even with carry. But careful: max over u,v of A_u B_v + A_{u+m}B_{v-1} could be up to 2 α_A α_B if α occurs in both A_u/A_{u+m} and B_v/B_{v-1}. So bound R≤R_A R_B/2. If we can find any sequence A with R_A<2, recursively tensor product reduces objective! For example A length 2 with optimal R=16/9≈1.777. Tensor two would upper bound (1.777^2)/2=1.581 > target but <2. Tensor three: (1.777^3)/2^2=1.406! This is deterministic! But are we allowed to choose length N=m^K and p Kronecker of high-R sequence? It might achieve R below target. Need verify exact maximum of Kronecker convolution. This is huge insight.

Let's test with small m=2 optimal sequence a=[1/3,2/3]? R=16/9. Kronecker length4: p_{i+2j}=a_i b_j with a=b. Values:
a0=1/3, a1=2/3. p=[1/9,2/9,2/9,4/9]. Sum=1. Compute convolution? Let's do to see objective.

Need explore exact formula for Kronecker of a small sequence could be a deterministic path to target.

Let's analyze Kronecker more generally. Suppose A length m, B length K. p_{i+m j}=a_i b_j. Full convolution coefficient: C_{u,v}=A_u B_v + A_{u+m} B_{v-1}. This is like convolution in a "half product". Let define a 2D sequence? The length N=mK. Objective R_p = 2mK max C / (SaSb)^2.
If we choose A and B independently, can we derive recurrence for optimal R? Let normalize Sa=Sb=1. We need minimize max_{u,v} [A_u B_v + A_{u+m} B_{v-1}]. Since B length 2K-1. This max is at most max_u A_min? Not independent. If we choose A and B such that A_u and A_{u+m} and B_v/B_{v-1} balanced, maybe max lower. This resembles designing 2D mass with diagonal sums.

For m=2, A_u for u=0,1 and A_{u+2} for u=0? Actually A length2, conv length3 indices 0,1,2. For u=0: A0 B0 + A2 B_{-1}? B_{-1}=0 => A0B0. u=1: A1 B1 + A3B0 (A3=0) => A1B1. Wait formula with v; max C occurs maybe u? Let's list for p=a0b0, a1b0, a0b1,a1b1. Conv coefficients correspond to sums of two-digit numbers with base2 carries. There are at most? In our example max at c4 (u=0,v=2?) and c1 etc.
Actually coefficient includes both lower sum carry; maybe not a simple product of A coefficients? It is exactly.

Could recursively use m=2 optimal A and B same to get length 2^r. This is like distributions over digits with weights. Let's compute for r=1 (length2) R=16/9≈1.777. r=2 (length4) R=160/81≈1.975 > R1? So tensor of nonuniform increases >2. Not good. What about choose A with lower R? minimum length2 1.777. Tensor not promising.

What about 2D independent mapping with no carry using large base? We saw stride increases n, objective scales with n, bad. Kronecker base small causes carries but length compact. The actual R could be below product, but for length4 1.975 close to2. Not target.

Another construction: Use **cyclic convolution** with length n? If sums wrap, we could design binary with perfect autocorrelation. But linear doesn't wrap. Could append zeros to avoid wrap? If use cyclic length N = mK and then cut? Not.

Let's return to optimization.

But the Kronecker observation suggests global optimization may be complex. Our Fourier equalization should handle.

Can we exploit **2D separable with no carry** by making length N=mK and using base K but lower component has support less than base/2 so no carry? If lower length a < m, upper length K, indices i + m j with i<a, no carry? But n still mK, many zeros, objective factor large; not good. A 2D grid cannot map injectively to [0,N-1] without carry unless use base B max lower+upper <B and fill all lower positions 0..B-1; then a=B. Carry inevitable for sums.

What about using **digits with negative values?** no.

Top-level docstring.
Imports: `import time, numpy as np, sys`. `try: from scipy.optimize import linprog except: linprog=None`. `try: import scipy.sparse as sp except: sp=None`.

Helpers:
```
def _normalize(pos, floor=1e-300):
    x = np.asarray(pos, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if x.size==0: return np.ones(1)
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    x = np.maximum(x, 0.0)
    s = x.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        x = np.ones(x.size)
        s = x.sum()
    return x / s
```
Need preserve length. If zero length? not.

`fft_length(n1, n2)`.
```
def next_power_of_two(m):
    L=1
    while L < m: L <<=1
    return L
```
```
def full_conv_fft(a,b=None):
    if b is None: b=a
    a=np.asarray(a); b=np.asarray(b)
    n=a.size; m=b.size
    if n==0 or m==0: return np.zeros(1)
    L=next_power_of_two(n+m-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    if b is a:
       fb=fa
    else: fb=np.fft.rfft(b,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,L)
    return c[:n+m-1]
```
But if b is a, `fa*fa` complex? For real conv of a with a, using same FFT okay. For real linear conv length L. Good. For `b` not a, product.
Potential numerical tiny negative; callers handle.

`hard_score_fft(a)`:
```
s=a.sum()
if s<=1e-300: return np.inf
b=full_conv_fft(a,a)
mx=b.max()
if not finite or mx<0: mx=abs(mx?)
return 2.0*a.size*mx/(s*s)
```
Because a may not normalized. For normalized, s=1.

`soft_score_p(p, tau)`: p normalized; but use general.
```
def soft_score_p(p, tau):
    n=p.size; b=full_conv_fft(p,p); M=b.max(); if not finite...; z=(b-M)/tau; z=np.clip(z, -50, 50); Z=z.max()+np.log(np.sum(np.exp(z-z.max()))); return 2*n*(M+tau*Z)
```
Actually logsumexp(b/tau)= (M/tau)+log∑exp((b-M)/tau); Msoft=M+tau*... yes.

`grad_conv_from_q(p,q)`:
Input p length n, q length 2n-1. Use FFT length L≥3n-2? But one can reuse L for p*p? We'll compute cross-correlation. Need ensure p maybe not normalized? Doesn't matter. q sum maybe not 1. For gradient of linear combination of b with weights q (not normalized), `g_i=∑ q_k p_{k-i}`. So if q weights not normalized, formula holds. For soft, q normalized sum1. For active subgradient, q uniform sum1.
Implementation with conj:
```
def grad_conv_q(p,q):
    n=p.size
    L=next_power_of_two(3*n-2)  # for cross-correlation? But if we use conjugate and q length 2n-1, linear cross-correlation length 3n-2. Need L≥3n-2.
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,L)
    Q=np.fft.rfft(q,L)
    rate=np.fft.irfft(P*np.conj(Q), L)
    g=np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    g[0]=rate[0]
    if n>1:
       g[1:]=rate[L-1:L-1-(n-1):-1]
    return g
```
Check for q length <L; zero pad. The rate index for lag -i: rate[(L - (i % L)) % L]. For i=0 index0; i>0 L-i. Our slice: start L-1, stop L-1-(n-1) exclusive? For n=3: L-1 down to L-3? `rate[L-1:L-3:-1]` gives indices L-1,L-2 length2. Good. For n=1, only g[0].
Need ensure real: `rate = rate.real`.

But `P*np.conj(Q)` length via rfft? `P` and `Q` are rfft arrays length L/2+1. Multiplying works. `irfft` real.

For q of length `2n-1`, using `np.conj(Q)` gives correlation c_m =∑ p_j q_{j-m}? We derived g_i=rate[L-i]. Let's test with example mentally maybe. If p=[1,0] q=[0,1,0]. Desired g0=q1*p0? i=0: k=1 p1=0? Wait p=[p0=1,p1=0]; q [0,1,0]. g0= q0 p0 + q1 p1 =0; g1=q1 p0 + q2 p1=1. Our formula: circular correlation rate[d]=∑ p_j q_{j-d}. L≥4. rate[0]=p0q0+p1q1=0. rate[L-1] (d=-1)=p0q1+p1q2=1. Good. Works.

`subgradient_polish`:
```
def subgradient_polish(p, time_budget=10.0, eta0=0.15, active_eps0=0.05, active_eps_final=0.001, max_iters=100000):
    p=_normalize(p); start=...
    best=p; best_score=hard_score_fft(p)
    eta=eta0; active_eps=active_eps0; no_improve=0
    while time<deadline and iters<max:
       b=full_conv_fft(p,p); m=b.max()
       if not finite break
       # active mask: b >= m*(1-active_eps) and maybe top quantile
       mask = b >= m*(1-active_eps)
       if mask.sum()==0: mask[int(m)]=True
       q = np.zeros_like(b); q[mask]=1.0/mask.sum()
       g=grad_conv_q(p,q)
       # make g centered? For softmax? Multiplicative no.
       best_cand=p; best_sc=best_score
       # scale g to p scale for eta? g magnitude roughly p; eta dimensionless.
       for cand_eta in (eta, eta*0.5, eta*0.2, eta*0.05):
           denom = p + 1e-300
           z = -cand_eta * g / denom
           # clip z to prevent extreme
           z = np.clip(z, -20.0, 20.0)
           z -= z.max()
           p2 = np.exp(z); s=p2.sum()
           if s>0: p2=p2/s
           else continue
           sc=hard_score_fft(p2)
           if sc < best_sc: best_sc=sc; best_cand=p2
       if best_sc < best_score:
           p=best_cand; best=p.copy(); best_score=best_sc
           no_improve=0
           eta=min(2.0, eta*1.1)
           if active_eps > active_eps_final: active_eps = max(active_eps_final, active_eps*0.98)
       else:
           no_improve +=1
           eta *= 0.5
           if eta < 1e-4:
              eta=0.05
              if no_improve > 20:
                 active_eps = max(active_eps_final, active_eps*0.8)
           if no_improve > 200: break
       # occasional exact? no
    return best, best_score
```
Potential issue: multiplicative update with z=-eta g/p changes all p; if g has zero for many, okay. But if g has negative? Convolution of nonnegative p and q nonnegative, so g nonnegative. So z≤0; update only decreases some p, then normalization increases all relatively. This is fine: subgradient to reduce active contributions. It may drive p_i to zero if g_i large. Could cause loss of support. But active set changes. Use eta small. Maybe we should **skip** if update score worsens. Good.

Add a symmetric averaging move? Since sequence reversal same score; subgradient on p and reverse maybe. We can symmetrize occasionally: p = 0.5(p+rev)/sum. This may improve? Reversal has same B reversed; average may reduce max. In subgradient loop, every 50 iterations test sym. Similarly mirror with shifted? We'll add in block search.

`fourier_equalize` details:
```
def fourier_equalize(p, iters=2000, time_budget=10.0, lam=0.2, log_clip=0.6, mix=0.5, target_mode='clip', peak_factor=0.98, flat_mix=0.1):
   p=_normalize(p); n=p.size; L=next_power_of_two(2*n)
   q_flat=np.full(L, 1.0/(2*n-1))? only first 2n-1. Actually total length 2n-1, q_flat first 2n-1 ones/(2n-1), rest zero.
   p_best=p; score_best=hard_score_fft(p)
   start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+max(0,time_budget)
   for it in range(iters):
      if time over: break
      P=np.fft.rfft(p,L)
      B=np.fft.irfft(P*P, L).real
      B=np.where(B>0,B,0.0)
      Bsum=B[:2*n-1].sum()
      if Bsum<=0: break
      Bn=B/Bsum
      if target_mode=='clip':
          T=Bn.max()*peak_factor
          high=Bn > T
          excess=np.sum(Bn[high]-T)
          Bn[high]=T
          low=~high
          if low.sum()>0:
              Bn[low] += excess / low.sum()
          Bn=Bn/Bn.sum()
          Q=(1.0-flat_mix)*Bn + flat_mix*q_flat
      else:
          Q=~? q_flat
      Qsum=Q.sum(); if Qsum>0: Q=Q/Qsum
      tiny=1e-300
      active=(Bn>tiny) | (Q>tiny)
      logB=np.zeros(L); logQ=np.zeros(L)
      logB[active]=np.log(Bn[active]+tiny)
      logQ[active]=np.log(Q[active]+tiny)
      delta=0.5*(logQ-logB); delta=np.clip(delta, -log_clip, log_clip)
      P=P*np.exp(delta)
      pnew=np.fft.irfft(P, L).real[:n]
      pnew=np.maximum(pnew,0.0)
      s=pnew.sum()
      if s>0: pnew/=s
      else: pnew=p
      p2=(1.0-mix)*p + mix*pnew
      p2=np.maximum(p2,0.0); s2=p2.sum(); if s2>0 p2/=s2 else p2=p
      if it%20==0 or it==iters-1:
          sc=hard_score_fft(p2)
          if sc<score_best: best...
      p=p2
   # final check
   sc=hard_score_fft(p); update
   return best, score_best
```
Need careful: In target_clip, we modify Bn in place; q_flat unaffected. The target Q after clipping has sum1. But Q may have zero in many bins; logQ -inf -> delta negative? active logic with Q=0 and B>0: logQ=0? We set logQ[active]=log(Q+tiny), so logQ≈log(tiny) very negative; delta negative, P reduced where B high but Q zero. Good. Where Q>0, B=0: logB tiny negative, delta large positive clipped, increases P. Should be fine.
But if Q flat has all positive, active all first 2n-1; no zeros beyond. For Q first 2n-1 only, rest zero. For B beyond 2n-1 zero (should be). Good.

Mix parameter: If mix=1, jump. We can schedule mix: start high 0.8, later lower. In function, perhaps use `mix` fixed. We can call multiple stages with different mix/log_clip.

Call fourier_equalize with parameters schedule from outside:
```
def fourier_schedule(p, time_budget):
   p1, sc1 = fourier_equalize(p, time_budget=time_budget/2, lam=0.3, log_clip=0.8, mix=0.7, target_mode='clip', peak_factor=0.95, flat_mix=0.2)
   p2, sc2 = fourier_equalize(p1, time_budget=time_budget/4, lam=0.15, log_clip=0.4, mix=0.5, peak_factor=0.98, flat_mix=0.1)
   p3, sc3 = fourier_equalize(p2, time_budget=time_budget/4, lam=0.05, log_clip=0.15, mix=0.3, peak_factor=0.995, flat_mix=0.05)
   return best
```
Maybe do inside.

`block_search` details:
```
def block_search(p, time_budget=20.0, seed=0):
 rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
 p=_normalize(p); best...; sigma=0.1
 no=0
 while time:
   cand=p.copy()
   n=len(cand)
   mode=rng.random()
   if mode<0.6:
      width=max(2,int(n*rng.uniform(0.002,0.15)))
      start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1))
      factor=float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,sigma)))
      cand[start:start+width]*=factor
   elif mode<0.8:
      # symmetrize with reverse
      w=float(rng.uniform(0.2,0.8))
      cand = w*cand + (1-w)*cand[::-1]
   else:
      # sinusoidal modulation
      amp=float(rng.uniform(0.01,0.15)*sigma*10?)
      freq=float(rng.uniform(1,8))
      phase=float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
      mod=1.0+amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*freq*np.arange(n)/n + phase)
      cand*=mod
   cand=np.maximum(cand,0); s=cand.sum(); if s>0 cand/=s
   sc=hard_score_fft(cand)
   if sc<best_score:
      p=cand; best...; no=0; sigma=min(0.5, sigma*1.05)
   else:
      no+=1; sigma=max(0.01, sigma*0.95)
      if no>200: break
 return best,best_score
```
This might be slow if many exact conv but fine. Use `hard_score_fft` not exact. Could also use a fast approximate score: max of convolution sampled? But full FFT fine.

`resize_profile`:
```
def resize_profile(p, n):
   p=np.asarray(p).ravel(); m=p.size
   if m==n: return _normalize(p)
   x_old=np.arange(m, dtype=float); x_new=np.linspace(0, m-1, n)
   val=np.interp(x_new, x_old, p)
   return _normalize(val)
```

Generate analytic using index arrays:
```
i=np.arange(n); x=(i+0.5)/n
# left powers
for a in [0.5,0.55,0.6,0.7,0.8]:
  starts.append(_normalize((i+1.0)**(a-1.0)))
  starts.append(_normalize((n-i)**(a-1.0)))
  starts.append(_normalize((i+1.0)**(a-1.0) * ((n-i+1.0) ** (0.5-a?))?))
# Beta grid: use x and 1-x with eps
eps=1e-6
for a in [0.5,0.75,1.0,1.5]:
 for b in [0.5,0.75,1.0,1.5]:
   val=x**(a-1.0)*(1-x)**(b-1.0)
   if finite and sum: starts.append(_normalize(val))
# uniform
# random smooth
for _ in range(6):
   M=16
   theta=rng.normal(0,1,M)
   t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   val=1.0
   for m in range(1,M+1):
      val += 0.4/m * theta[m-1]*np.cos(m*np.pi*t)  # DCT half-range
   val=np.maximum(val,1e-4)
   starts.append(_normalize(val))
# random power with random exponent and mirror weight
for _ in range(6):
   a=rng.uniform(0.45,1.0)
   w=rng.uniform(0,1)
   L=(i+1)**(a-1); R=(n-i)**(a-1)
   val=(1-w)*L + w*R; val[max]
   starts.append(_normalize(val))

LP matrix building: Use `np.tile(f, n)` where f length n; data length n*n. For n=256, 65536 okay. Rows/cols cached. Row indices int32 maybe to save memory; use `np.int32`? For n=256 max row <511, int32 okay. But scipy sparse accepts int64/int32. Use int32 for speed/memory. If n maybe512, max1023 int32. Good.
```
def get_lp_indices(n):
    cache = LP_CACHE
    if n in cache: return cache[n]
    cols = np.tile(np.arange(n, dtype=np.int32), n)
    row_offsets = np.repeat(np.arange(n, dtype=np.int32), n)
    rows = (row_offsets + np.tile(np.arange(n, dtype=np.int32), n)).astype(np.int32)
    cache[n]=(rows,cols)
    return rows,cols
```
Rows total n*n. `np.repeat(arange(n), n)` gives [0,0,...,1,1]. `np.tile(arange(n), n)` offsets [0..n-1 repeat]. Good.

`lp_next`:
```
def lp_next(f, M, timeout=10.0):
    if linprog is None or sp is None: return None
    n=f.size
    rows,cols=get_lp_indices(n)
    data=np.tile(f, n)
    A=sp.csr_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(2*n-1, n))
    c=-np.ones(n)
    b=np.full(2*n-1, float(M))
    bounds=[(0.0,None) for _ in range(n)]
    try:
       res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'time_limit': max(1.0,float(timeout)), 'output_flag': False})
    except TypeError:
       res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
    except Exception:
       return None
    if res is None or not hasattr(res,'x') or res.x is None: return None
    g=np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)): return None
    g=np.maximum(g,0.0)
    if g.sum()<=0: return None
    # feasibility check
    conv=full_conv_fft(f,g)
    viol=float(np.max(conv - M)) if conv.size else 0
    if not np.isfinite(viol) or viol > max(1e-6, 1e-4*M):
       # try small projection? If slightly high, scale g down? If f*g has max >M, multiply g by (M/(max+eps))?
       mx=float(np.max(conv))
       if mx > 1e-300:
          g = g * (M / mx)
          gsum=g.sum()
          if gsum>0: g/=gsum? Wait scaling changes bound? If f*g max becomes M, but sum? We can normalize after? Normalizing increases conv, may violate. The LP solution should represent max sum; if we scale down to satisfy, then normalize for update may violate but step small. For acceptance, maybe discard if violation large. If slight, scale down then normalize? Not preserving feasible. We can just normalize the (maybe infeasible) g for direction; but LP solution better. If violation > tolerance, discard.
       else: return None
    gsum=g.sum()
    if gsum<=1e-300: return None
    return g/gsum
```
But if we scale down to M, sum decreases; to use direction we normalize, which undoes bound. Feasibility check after scaling? Not needed. For LP direction, any feasible scaled direction; if we normalize, not feasible but convex update small. Maybe if violation due numerical, normalizing okay? Let's just discard if violation >1e-5*(M+1e-300). Because if LP returns slightly infeasible, f*g max M*(1+eps). Normalizing could make max >M? Direction still maybe okay. Now, let's think of potential performance bottlenecks:
- `generate_starts` for n=2048, many beta powers with `x**(a-1)`. x=0.5/n to 1-0.5/n. finite. Loops M=16 for random, okay.
- `fourier_equalize`: In each iteration, computing `logB` and `logQ` full L (2048/4096). Fine. But creating arrays each iter overhead. Could optimize but okay.
- `subgradient_polish`: each iteration computes b, grad, then candidate scores (3 hard conv). Could be heavy if many. Need time budget. Use no too high max_iters.
- `block_search`: candidate scores one conv each, can run many. Good.

We should not use `np.exp` on large z in Fourier many times; delta clipped <=log_clip (0.8), exp small.

`fourier_schedule` can produce p with sum not exactly due clipping; normalize.

Let's write `fourier_schedule`:
```
def fourier_schedule(p, time_budget=20.0):
    p=_normalize(p)
    if time_budget <=0: return p, hard_score_fft(p)
    t1=time.monotonic()
    dur=max(1.0, time_budget*0.45)
    p1,_=fourier_equalize(p, time_budget=dur, log_clip=0.9, mix=0.7, target_mode='clip', peak_factor=0.95, flat_mix=0.25)
    if time.monotonic()-t1 > time_budget: return p1, hard_score_fft(p1)
    dur2=max(1.0, min(time_budget*0.35, max(0, time_budget-(time.monotonic()-t1))))
    p2,_=fourier_equalize(p1, time_budget=dur2, log_clip=0.45, mix=0.45, target_mode='clip', peak_factor=0.98, flat_mix=0.12)
    dur3=max(0.5, max(0, time_budget-(time.monotonic()-t1-dur2)))
    p3,_=fourier_equalize(p2, time_budget=dur3, log_clip=0.18, mix=0.25, target_mode='clip', peak_factor=0.995, flat_mix=0.05)
    # choose best among p1,p2,p3
    best=p; best_sc=hard_score_fft(p)
    for cand in (p1,p2,p3):
       sc=hard_score_fft(cand)
       if sc<best_sc: best=cand; best_sc=sc
    return best, best_sc
```

def soft_jacobian_x(x, tau):
    p=softmax_x(x)
    b=full_conv_fft(p,p)
    M=b.max()
    if not np.isfinite(M): return zeros
    z=(b-M)/tau
    z=np.where(np.isfinite(z), z, 0.0)
    z=z-z.max()
    # clip to avoid overflow
    z=np.clip(z, -50, 50)
    q=np.exp(z); qsum=q.sum(); if qsum>0 q/=qsum else q=np.zeros_like(b)
    g=grad_conv_q(p,q)
    gv=float(np.dot(p,g))
    grad=np.empty(len(x), dtype=np.float64)
    if grad.size: grad=p[:-1]*(g[:-1]-gv)
    return grad
```

Let's write exact code snippets for each:

```
def _normalize(p, floor=0.0):
    arr = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        arr = np.ones(arr.size, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(arr.size)
    return arr / s
```
No floor. If p has negative, clipped.

`hard_score_fft`:
```
def hard_score_fft(a):
   a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
   n=a.size
   if n==0: return np.inf
   s=float(a.sum())
   if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300: return np.inf
   b=full_conv_fft(a,a)
   if b.size==0: return np.inf
   mx=float(np.max(b))
   if not np.isfinite(mx): return np.inf
   if mx < 0.0:
       # numerical, assume tiny
       mx = max(0.0, mx)
   return float(2.0*n*mx/(s*s))
```
If all b negative due numerical, mx=0 gives 0 score impossible; But nonnegative p conv nonnegative. If p all positive, b positive. If due FFT error all negative impossible. But if p very small (sum normalized) no.

`full_conv_fft`:
```
def full_conv_fft(a,b=None):
    a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if b is None: b=a
    else: b=np.asarray(b,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size; m=b.size
    if n==0 or m==0: return np.zeros(1,dtype=np.float64)
    L=1
    target=n+m-1
    while L < target: L <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    if b is a:
        fb=fa
    else:
        fb=np.fft.rfft(b,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,L)
    return c[:target]
```
If `b` is a but b variable rebound? `if b is a` after b may be same object if a input same. In call `full_conv_fft(a,a)`, b is a. In `lp_next` `full_conv_fft(f,g)` different. Good. If `a` and `b` equal values but different objects, uses two FFT; okay.

Could `fa*fa` produce complex fa times fa, for real p, P^2 correct. For `b` different, product. Good.

`grad_conv_q`:
```
def grad_conv_q(p,q):
    p=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64).ravel(); q=np.asarray(q,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=p.size
    if n==0: return np.zeros(0)
    # q length should 2n-1; if not, pad/truncate? 
    expected=2*n-1
    qq=np.zeros(expected, dtype=np.float64)
    q_use = min(q.size, expected)
    qq[:q_use] = q[:q_use]
    if q.size > expected: qq += q[expected:]? not expected. Use only first.
    L=1
    target=3*n-2
    while L<target: L <<=1
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,L)
    Q=np.fft.rfft(qq,L)
    rate=np.fft.irfft(P*np.conj(Q), L)
    g=np.empty(n,dtype=np.float64)
    if n>0: g[0]=float(rate[0])

`block_search`: Include random seed arg. Use `rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)`. Time loop. Use inside exact? no.
```
def block_search(p, time_budget=20.0, seed=0):
    p=_normalize(p); n=p.size
    start=...; deadline=...
    best=p; best_sc=hard_score_fft(p)
    eta? sigma=0.12; no=0
    iteration=0
    while time<deadline:
       iteration+=1
       cand = p.copy()
       r=rng.random()
       if r<0.55:
          width=max(2, int(n*rng.uniform(0.003,0.12)))
          starti=int(rng.integers(0,max(1,n-width+1)))
          factor=float(np.exp(rng.normal(0, sigma)))
          cand[starti:starti+width] *= factor
       elif r<0.75:
          w=float(rng.uniform(0.2,0.8))
          cand = w*cand + (1-w)*cand[::-1]
       elif r<0.9:
          # low-frequency sinusoid
          amp=float(sigma*0.3 + rng.uniform(0.0,0.05))
          freq=float(rng.uniform(1.0,6.0))
          phase=float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2*np.pi))
          mod = 1.0 + amp * np.cos((2.0*np.pi*freq)*np.arange(n)/float(n)+phase)
          cand *= np.maximum(mod, 0.1)
       else:
          # random smooth block gain
          width=max(3, int(n*rng.uniform(0.02,0.25)))
          starti=int(rng.integers(0,max(1,n-width+1)))
          g = np.exp(rng.normal(0, sigma, width))
          # smooth g by simple average? 
          cand[starti:starti+width] *= g
       cand=np.maximum(cand,0.0); s=cand.sum()
       if s > 1e-300: cand=cand/s
       sc=hard_score_fft(cand)
       if sc < best_sc:
          p=cand; best=p.copy(); best_sc=sc; no=0; sigma=min(0.4, sigma*1.02)
       else:
          no += 1
          if no == 10: sigma=max(0.01, sigma*0.8)
          if no > 300: break
    return best,best_sc
```

    # parse budget
    if budget_s is None: budget_s=2400.0
    for key in ('budget_s','budget','time','time_budget'):

`lp_local_search` should use full_conv_fft and hard_score. It can call `lp_next`. We must pass `time_budget` and `timeout_per`. It loops.
```
def lp_local_search(f, time_budget=60.0, max_iters=20, timeout_per=10.0):
    f=_normalize(f); best=f.copy(); best_sc=hard_score_fft(f)
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+max(0,time_budget)
    f_cur=f.copy()
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       b=full_conv_fft(f_cur,f_cur); M=float(np.max(b))
       if M <=0: break
       g=lp_next(f_cur, M, timeout=timeout_per)
       if g is None: break
       best_cand=f_cur; best_cand_sc=best_sc
       # but if current f_cur maybe not best? use f_cur
       for t in (0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0):
           cand=(1.0-t)*f_cur + t*g
           cand=_normalize(cand)
           sc=hard_score_fft(cand)
           if sc < best_cand_sc:
              best_cand_sc=sc; best_cand=cand
       if best_cand_sc < best_sc:
           f_cur=best_cand; best=best_cand.copy(); best_sc=best_cand_sc
       else:
           # try t=0.05? included? 0.1. If no, break after maybe 2
           if it >= 2:
              break
    return best, best_sc
```
Maybe LP direction not improve first due convex update too large; we can include t=0.05. List `(0.05,0.1,0.25,0.5,0.8,1.0)`.

`register_candidate`:
```
def register_candidate(state, p, compute_exact=True):
    p = _normalize(p)
    if p.size == 0:
        return
    sur = hard_score_fft(p)
    if not np.isfinite(sur):
        return
    if sur < state.get('best_sur', np.inf):
        state['best_sur'] = sur
        state['sur_p'] = p.copy()
    if compute_exact:
        try:
            ex = float(evaluate_sequence(p.tolist()))
        except Exception:
            ex = sur
        if np.isfinite(ex):
            if ex < state.get('best_exact', np.inf):
                state['best_exact'] = ex
                state['best_p'] = p.copy()
```
If exact is lower than sur due to evaluator clipping? Evaluator clips to [0,1000]; we are within. It also if sum<0.01 inf. sum1. exact should close. Keep min.

`finalize_candidate`:
```
def finalize_candidate(state, deadline=None):
    candidates=[]
    for key in ('best_p','sur_p'):
       p=state.get(key)
       if p is not None:
          candidates.append(np.asarray(p).ravel())
    best_p=None; best_ex=np.inf
    for p in candidates:
       p=_normalize(p)
       try: ex=float(evaluate_sequence(p.tolist()))
       except: ex=np.inf
       if np.isfinite(ex) and ex < best_ex:
          best_p=p; best_ex=ex
    if best_p is None:
       best_p=_normalize(np.ones(1024))
       try: best_ex=float(evaluate_sequence(best_p.tolist()))
       except: best_ex=2.0
    # ensure sum and bounds
    arr = [float(max(0.0, min(1000.0, x))) for x in best_p]
    s=sum(arr)
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
       arr = [1.0 for _ in range(1024)]
    return arr
```

```

```python
"""
Searches for non-negative sequences minimizing 2*n*max(conv(a,a))/sum(a)^2.

The main ideas are:
  1. Work with normalized sequences summing to 1; the score is scale invariant.
  2. Use a fast FFT surrogate for convolution and maximum coefficient.
  3. Fourier equalization: iteratively move the squared FFT spectrum toward a
     flat target convolution profile, then project back to non-negative density.
  4. Subgradient polish on currently active largest convolution coefficients.
  5. Optional LP alternating step from the suggested state-of-art idea.
  6. Length refinement and block/local search for final polish.
"""

import time
import numpy as np

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog as _sp_linprog
except Exception:
    _sp_linprog = None

try:
    import scipy.sparse as _sp_sparse
except Exception:
    _sp_sparse = None

_LP_CACHE = {}


def _normalize(p):
    """Return a finite non-negative float array with sum 1."""
    arr = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        arr = np.ones(arr.size, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(arr.size)
    return arr / s


def _next_power_of_two(m):
    L = 1
    while L < int(m):
        L <<= 1
    return L


def _full_conv_fft(a, b=None):
    """Full linear convolution using FFT."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if b is None:
        b = a
    else:
        b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    m = b.size
    if n == 0 or m == 0:
        return np.zeros(1, dtype=np.float64)
    L = _next_power_of_two(n + m - 1)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    if b is a:
        fb = fa
    else:
        fb = np.fft.rfft(b, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fb, L)
    return c[:n + m - 1]


def _hard_score_fft(a):
    """Fast score using FFT convolution. Matches evaluate_sequence for finite inputs."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.inf
    b = _full_conv_fft(a, a)
    if b.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    mx = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return np.inf
    if mx < 0.0:
        mx = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * mx / (s * s))


def _grad_given_weights(p, q):
    """
    Gradient of sum_k q_k * (p*p)_k wrt p_i.
    For_i in [0,n-1], g_i = sum_k q_k p_{k-i}, zero outside support.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    expected = 2 * n - 1
    qq = np.zeros(expected, dtype=np.float64)
    take = min(q.size, expected)
    if take > 0:
        qq[:take] = q[:take]
    L = _next_power_of_two(3 * n - 2)
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    Q = np.fft.rfft(qq, L)
    rate = np.fft.irfft(P * np.conj(Q), L)
    g = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    g[0] = float(rate[0])
    if n > 1:
        g[1:] = rate[L - 1:L - 1 - (n - 1):-1]
    g[np.logical_not(np.isfinite(g))] = 0.0
    return np.maximum(g, 0.0)


def _generate_starts(n, rng, n_random=6):
    """Generate diverse normalized starting profiles of length n."""
    starts = []
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    x = (i + 0.5) / float(n)
    one_minus_x = 1.0 - x
    eps = 1e-4 / float(max(1, n))

    # Global previous construction, if available.
    try:
        g = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
        if g is not None:
            ga = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            if ga.size > 1:
                ga = np.where(np.isfinite(ga), ga, 0.0)
                ga = np.maximum(ga, 0.0)
                if float(ga.sum()) > 0.0:
                    starts.append(_resize_profile(ga, n))
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Uniform.
    starts.append(_normalize(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)))

    # Power laws from left and right.
    for a in (0.5, 0.55, 0.6, 0.7, 0.8):
        left = (i + 1.0) ** (a - 1.0)
        right = (n - i) ** (a - 1.0)
        starts.append(_normalize(left))
        starts.append(_normalize(right))
        starts.append(_normalize(0.7 * left + 0.3 * right))
        starts.append(_normalize(0.3 * left + 0.7 * right))

    # Beta densities.
    xs = np.maximum(x, eps)
    ys = np.maximum(one_minus_x, eps)
    for a in (0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.5):
        for b in (0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.5):
            val = xs ** (a - 1.0) * ys ** (b - 1.0)
            if np.all(np.isfinite(val)) and float(val.sum()) > 0.0:
                starts.append(_normalize(val))

    # Smooth DCT-like random shapes.
    t = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for _ in range(max(0, int(n_random))):
        modes = 12
        theta = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=modes)
        val = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for m in range(1, modes + 1):
            val += (0.35 / float(m)) * theta[m - 1] * np.cos(m * np.pi * t / float(n))
        val = np.maximum(val, 1e-5)
        starts.append(_normalize(val))

    # Random endpoint power mixtures.
    for _ in range(max(0, int(n_random))):
        a = float(rng.uniform(0.48, 1.05))
        w = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0))
        left = (i + 1.0) ** (a - 1.0)
        right = (n - i) ** (a - 1.0)
        val = (1.0 - w) * left + w * right
        starts.append(_normalize(val))

    # Keep only sane starts.
    good = []
    for p in starts:
        p = _normalize(p)
        if p.size == n and np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
            good.append(p)
    return good


def _resize_profile(p, n):
    """Resample/interpolate a profile to new length n."""
    p = _normalize(p)
    m = p.size
    if m == n:
        return p
    if m < 2:
        return _normalize(np.full(max(1, int(n)), float(p[0]) if m else 1.0))
    x_old = np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, float(m - 1), int(n))
    val = np.interp(x_new, x_old, p)
    return _normalize(val)


def _register_candidate(state, p, compute_exact=True):
    """Update best surrogate and best exact candidates in a state dict."""
    p = _normalize(p)
    if p.size == 0:
        return
    sur = _hard_score_fft(p)
    if not np.isfinite(sur):
        return
    if sur < state.get("best_sur", np.inf):
        state["best_sur"] = float(sur)
        state["sur_p"] = p.copy()
    if compute_exact:
        try:
            ex = float(evaluate_sequence(p.tolist()))
        except Exception:
            ex = sur
        if np.isfinite(ex):
            if ex < state.get("best_exact", np.inf):
                state["best_exact"] = float(ex)
                state["best_p"] = p.copy()


def _fourier_equalize(p, time_budget=10.0, log_clip=0.5, mix=0.5,
                      peak_factor=0.98, flat_mix=0.1):
    """
    Iteratively push the full convolution profile toward a truncated-flat target.
    This is a projected frequency-domain Gauss-Newton style update.
    """
    p = _normalize(p)
    n = p.size
    if time_budget <= 0.0:
        return p, _hard_score_fft(p)
    L = _next_power_of_two(2 * n)
    q_flat = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    q_flat[:2 * n - 1] = 1.0 / float(2 * n - 1)

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(time_budget))
    best = p.copy()
    best_score = _hard_score_fft(p)
    tiny = 1e-300

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        P = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
        B = np.fft.irfft(P * P, L).real
        B = np.where(B > 0.0, B, 0.0)
        Bsum = float(np.sum(B[:2 * n - 1]))
        if Bsum <= tiny:
            break
        Bn = B / Bsum

        score = 2.0 * float(n) * float(np.max(Bn[:2 * n - 1]))
        if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
            best_score = float(score)
            best = p.copy()

        max_b = float(np.max(Bn[:2 * n - 1]))
        if max_b <= tiny:
            break

        T = max_b * float(peak_factor)
        arr = Bn.copy()
        high = arr > T
        if np.any(high):
            excess = float(np.sum(arr[high] - T))
            arr[high] = T
            low = ~high
            if low.sum() > 0 and excess > 0.0:
                arr[low] += excess / float(low.sum())
        arrsum = float(arr.sum())
        if arrsum > tiny:
            arr = arr / arrsum

        Q = (1.0 - float(flat_mix)) * arr + float(flat_mix) * q_flat
        Qsum = float(Q.sum())
        if Qsum <= tiny:
            break
        Q = Q / Qsum

        active = (Bn > tiny) | (Q > tiny)
        logB = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        logQ = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        logB[active] = np.log(Bn[active] + tiny)
        logQ[active] = np.log(Q[active] + tiny)
        delta = 0.5 * (logQ - logB)
        delta = np.where(np.isfinite(delta), delta, 0.0)
        delta = np.clip(delta, -float(log_clip), float(log_clip))

        P = P * np.exp(delta)
        pnew = np.fft.irfft(P, L).real[:n]
        pnew = np.where(np.isfinite(pnew), pnew, 0.0)
        pnew = np.maximum(pnew, 0.0)
        snew = float(pnew.sum())
        if snew <= tiny:
            continue
        pnew = pnew / snew

        p2 = (1.0 - mix) * p + mix * pnew
        p2 = np.where(np.isfinite(p2), p2, 0.0)
        p2 = np.maximum(p2, 0.0)
        s2 = float(p2.sum())
        if s2 <= tiny:
            continue
        p = p2 / s2

    final_score = _hard_score_fft(p)
    if final_score < best_score:
        best_score = final_score
        best = p.copy()
    return best, best_score


def _fourier_schedule(p, time_budget=25.0):
    """Run Fourier equalization with coarse-to-fine parameters."""
    p = _normalize(p)
    if time_budget <= 0.0:
        return p, _hard_score_fft(p)

    p1, s1 = _fourier_equalize(
        p,
        time_budget=max(1.0, 0.45 * time_budget),
        log_clip=0.9,
        mix=0.7,
        peak_factor=0.95,
        flat_mix=0.25,
    )
    remaining = time_budget - (time.monotonic() - time.monotonic())  # placeholder
    # Actual timing:
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    p1, s1 = _fourier_equalize(
        p,
        time_budget=max(1.0, 0.45 * time_budget),
        log_clip=0.9,
        mix=0.7,
        peak_factor=0.95,
        flat_mix=0.25,
    )
    elapsed = time.monotonic() - t0
    if elapsed < time_budget:
        p2, s2 = _fourier_equalize(
            p1,
            time_budget=max(0.5, min(0.35 * time_budget, time_budget - elapsed)),
            log_clip=0.45,
            mix=0.45,
            peak_factor=0.98,
            flat_mix=0.12,
        )
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - t0
    else:
        p2 = p1
        s2 = s1

    if time_budget - elapsed > 0.5:
        p3, s3 = _fourier_equalize(
            p2,
            time_budget=max(0.3, time_budget - elapsed),
            log_clip=0.18,
            mix=0.25,
            peak_factor=0.995,
            flat_mix=0.05,
        )
    else:
        p3 = p2
        s3 = s2

    best = p.copy()
    best_score = _hard_score_fft(p)
    for cand, sc in ((p1, s1), (p2, s2), (p3, s3)):
        cand = _normalize(cand)
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            sc = _hard_score_fft(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = float(sc)
            best = cand
    return best, float(best_score)


def _subgradient_polish(p, time_budget=10.0, eta0=0.18, active_eps0=0.05,
                        active_eps_min=0.002):
    """Subgradient polish on the near-maximum convolution coefficients."""
    p = _normalize(p)
    if time_budget <= 0.0:
        return p, _hard_score_fft(p)
    n = p.size
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(time_budget))
    best = p.copy()
    best_score = _hard_score_fft(p)
    eta = float(eta0)
    active_eps = float(active_eps0)
    floor = max(1e-14, 1e-8 / float(n))
    no_improve = 0
    improve_count = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        b = _full_conv_fft(p, p)
        m = float(np.max(b))
        if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 1e-300:
            break
        score = 2.0 * float(n) * m
        if score < best_score:
            best_score = float(score)
            best = p.copy()

        mask = b >= (m * (1.0 - active_eps))
        if not np.any(mask):
            idx = int(np.argmax(b))
            mask[idx] = True
        q = np.zeros_like(b, dtype=np.float64)
        q[mask] = 1.0 / float(mask.sum())
        g = _grad_given_weights(p, q)
        if not np.any(g):
            break

        best_cand = p
        best_cand_score = best_score
        for ce in (eta, 0.5 * eta, 0.1 * eta):
            z = -ce * g / (p + floor)
            z = np.where(np.isfinite(z), z, 0.0)
            z = np.clip(z, -50.0, 0.0)
            z -= float(z.max())
            p2 = np.exp(z)
            s2 = float(p2.sum())
            if s2 <= 1e-300:
                continue
            p2 = p2 / s2
            sc = _hard_score_fft(p2)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_cand_score:
                best_cand_score = float(sc)
                best_cand = p2

        if best_cand_score < best_score:
            p = best_cand
            best = p.copy()
            best_score = best_cand_score
            no_improve = 0
            improve_count += 1
            eta = min(1.6, eta * 1.05)
            if improve_count % 25 == 0:
                active_eps = max(active_eps_min, active_eps * 0.97)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            eta = max(1e-4, eta * 0.5)
            if no_improve == 40:
                active_eps = max(active_eps_min, active_eps * 0.85)
            if no_improve > 250:
                break

    final_score = _hard_score_fft(p)
    if final_score < best_score:
        best_score = float(final_score)
        best = p.copy()
    return best, float(best_score)


def _get_lp_indices(n):
    """Sparse index arrays for the convolution constraint matrix."""
    key = int(n)
    if key in _LP_CACHE:
        return _LP_CACHE[key]
    j = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int32)
    cols = np.repeat(j, n)
    row_offsets = np.repeat(j, n)
    offsets = np.tile(np.arange(n, dtype=np.int32), n)
    rows = (row_offsets + offsets).astype(np.int32)
    _LP_CACHE[key] = (rows, cols)
    return rows, cols


def _lp_next_dir(f, M, timeout_per=10.0):
    """
    Solve LP: max sum(g) subject to g>=0 and f*g <= M.
    Returns normalized feasible direction g (sum 1) or None.
    """
    if _sp_linprog is None or _sp_sparse is None:
        return None
    n = int(f.size)
    if n < 2:
        return None
    rows, cols = _get_lp_indices(n)
    data = np.tile(np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).ravel(), n)
    try:
        A = _sp_sparse.csr_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(2 * n - 1, n))
    except Exception:
        return None

    c = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b = np.full(2 * n - 1, float(M), dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, None) for _ in range(n)]

    try:
        res = _sp_linprog(
            c,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options={"time_limit": max(1.0, float(timeout_per)), "output_flag": False},
        )
    except TypeError:
        try:
            res = _sp_linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method="highs")
        except Exception:
            return None
    except Exception:
        return None

    if res is None or not hasattr(res, "x") or res.x is None:
        return None
    g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if g.size != n or not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
        return None
    g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
    if g.sum() <= 1e-300:
        return None

    conv = _full_conv_fft(f, g)
    viol = float(np.max(conv - M)) if conv.size else 0.0
    tol = max(1e-10, 1e-5 * float(M))
    if not np.isfinite(viol) or viol > tol:
        # If slightly infeasible, try a conservative per-coordinate scaling.
        mx = float(np.max(conv)) if conv.size else 0.0
        if mx > 1e-300:
            g = g * (float(M) / mx)
            conv2 = _full_conv_fft(f, g)
            viol2 = float(np.max(conv2 - M)) if conv2.size else 0.0
            if not np.isfinite(viol2) or viol2 > tol:
                return None
        else:
            return None

    g = g / g.sum()
    return np.maximum(g, 0.0)


def _lp_local_search(f, time_budget=60.0, max_iters=12, timeout_per=10.0):
    """Run alternating LP step for a few iterations."""
    f = _normalize(f)
    if time_budget <= 0.0:
        return f, _hard_score_fft(f)
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(time_budget))
    cur = f.copy()
    best = f.copy()
    best_score = _hard_score_fft(f)

    for it in range(max_iters):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        b = _full_conv_fft(cur, cur)
        M = float(np.max(b))
        if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 1e-300:
            break
        g = _lp_next_dir(cur, M, timeout_per=timeout_per)
        if g is None:
            break

        best_cand = cur.copy()
        best_cand_score = best_score
        for t in (0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.4, 0.7, 1.0):
            cand = (1.0 - t) * cur + t * g
            cand = _normalize(cand)
            sc = _hard_score_fft(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_cand_score:
                best_cand_score = float(sc)
                best_cand = cand

        if best_cand_score < best_score:
            cur = best_cand
            best = best_cand.copy()
            best_score = best_cand_score
        else:
            if it >= 2:
                break

    if best_score <= _hard_score_fft(f):
        return best, float(best_score)
    return f, float(_hard_score_fft(f))


def _block_search(p, time_budget=15.0, seed=0):
    """Simple stochastic local search with block/mirror/sinusoid moves."""
    p = _normalize(p)
    if time_budget <= 0.0:
        return p, _hard_score_fft(p)
    n = p.size
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(time_budget))
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    best = p.copy()
    best_score = _hard_score_fft(p)
    sigma = 0.12
    no_improve = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        cand = p.copy()
        r = float(rng.random())
        if r < 0.55:
            width = max(2, int(n * float(rng.uniform(0.003, 0.12))))
            lo = max(0, n - width)
            if lo < 1:
                st = 0
            else:
                st = int(rng.integers(0, lo + 1))
            factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
            cand[st:st + width] *= factor
        elif r < 0.72:
            w = float(rng.uniform(0.2, 0.8))
            cand = w * cand + (1.0 - w) * cand[::-1]
        elif r < 0.88:
            amp = float(sigma * 0.5 + rng.uniform(0.0, 0.04))
            freq = float(rng.uniform(1.0, 6.0))
            phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
            xx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
            mod = 1.0 + amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * freq * xx / float(n) + phase)
            mod = np.maximum(mod, 0.1)
            cand *= mod
        else:
            width = max(3, int(n * float(rng.uniform(0.02, 0.25))))
            lo = max(0, n - width)
            st = 0 if lo < 1 else int(rng.integers(0, lo + 1))
            gains = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=width))
            cand[st:st + width] *= gains

        cand = np.where(np.isfinite(cand), cand, 0.0)
        cand = np.maximum(cand, 0.0)
        s = float(cand.sum())
        if s <= 1e-300:
            continue
        cand = cand / s
        sc = _hard_score_fft(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            p = cand
            best = cand.copy()
            best_score = float(sc)
            no_improve = 0
            sigma = min(0.35, sigma * 1.03)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve == 8:
                sigma = max(0.015, sigma * 0.82)
            if no_improve > 350:
                break

    return best, float(best_score)


def _finalize_candidate(state, budget_s=2400.0):
    Pick the best exact candidate and return a valid list.
    candidates = []
    for key in ("best_p", "sur_p"):
        p = state.get(key, None)
        if p is not None:
            candidates.append(_normalize(p))

    best_p = None
    best_ex = np.inf
    for p in candidates:
        try:
            ex = float(evaluate_sequence(p.tolist()))
        except Exception:
            ex = np.inf
        if np.isfinite(ex) and ex < best_ex:
            best_ex = float(ex)
            best_p = p

    if best_p is None:
        best_p = _normalize(np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64))
        try:
            best_ex = float(evaluate_sequence(best_p.tolist()))
        except Exception:
            best_ex = 2.0

    out = [float(max(0.0, min(1000.0, x))) for x in best_p]
    s = float(sum(out))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
        out = [1.0 for _ in range(1024)]
    return out


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """Generate a strong non-negative sequence within the time budget."""
    if budget_s is None:
        budget_s = 2400.0
    for kk in ("budget_s", "budget", "time", "time_budget", "sec"):
        if kk in kwargs:
            try:
                budget_s = float(kwargs[kk])
                break
            except Exception:
                pass
    budget_s = max(30.0, float(budget_s))

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(20.0, float(budget_s) - 12.0)
    try:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    except Exception:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(12345)

    state = {
        "best_p": None,
        "best_exact": np.inf,
        "sur_p": None,
        "best_sur": np.inf,
    }

    # Safe baseline.
    base = _normalize(np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64))
    _register_candidate(state, base, compute_exact=True)
    print("initial upper bound =", state.get("best_exact"), flush=True)

    # Initial diverse starts.
    if budget_s > 900:
        NS = [512, 1024, 2048]
    elif budget_s > 350:
        NS = [256, 512, 1024]
    else:
        NS = [256, 512]

    profiles = []
    for N in NS:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 15.0:
            break
        starts = _generate_starts(N, rng, n_random=5 if N >= 1024 else 7)
        scored = []
        for p in starts:
            sc = _hard_score_fft(p)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                scored.append((float(sc), p))
        scored.sort()
        if scored:
            for _sc, p in scored[:5]:
                profiles.append(p)
            if len(scored) > 10:
                profiles.append(scored[len(scored) // 2][1])
            _register_candidate(state, scored[0][1], compute_exact=True)
        if len(profiles) > 18:
            break

    scored_profiles = []
    for p in profiles:
        sc = _hard_score_fft(p)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored_profiles.append((float(sc), p))
    scored_profiles.sort()
    profiles = [p for (_sc, p) in scored_profiles[:8]]

    # Medium optimization of most promising starting profiles.
    opt_profiles = profiles[:4]
    for idx, base_p in enumerate(opt_profiles):
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining < 30.0:
            break
        left = max(1, len(opt_profiles) - idx)
        budget = min(28.0, max(5.0, remaining / float(left)))

        p2, _s2 = _fourier_schedule(base_p, time_budget=budget)
        _register_candidate(state, p2, compute_exact=True)

        p3, _s3 = _subgradient_polish(p2, time_budget=min(9.0, max(1.0, 0.25 * budget)))
        _register_candidate(state, p3, compute_exact=True)

        p4, _s4 = _block_search(p3, time_budget=min(6.0, max(1.0, 0.20 * budget)),
                                seed=int(seed) + 17 * (idx + 1))
        _register_candidate(state, p4, compute_exact=True)

        opt_profiles.append(p4)

    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    print("after medium stage exact =", state.get("best_exact"),
          "sur =", state.get("best_sur"),
          "time left =", remaining, flush=True)

    # Optional LP stage on a modest length for shape-level improvement.
    if (_sp_linprog is not None and _sp_sparse is not None):
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining > 120.0:
            basep = state.get("sur_p", None)
            if basep is None:
                basep = profiles[0] if profiles else base
            plp = _resize_profile(basep, 256)
            plp, _ = _fourier_schedule(plp, time_budget=min(12.0, 0.10 * remaining))
            _register_candidate(state, plp, compute_exact=True)

            plp2, _ = _lp_local_search(plp, time_budget=min(70.0, 0.35 * remaining),
                                       timeout_per=8.0)
            _register_candidate(state, plp2, compute_exact=True)

            remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if remaining > 70.0:
                target = int(basep.size)
                if target < 1024:
                    target = 1024
                q = _resize_profile(plp2, target)
                q, _ = _fourier_schedule(q, time_budget=min(28.0, 0.45 * remaining))
                _register_candidate(state, q, compute_exact=True)
                q, _ = _subgradient_polish(q, time_budget=min(10.0, 0.20 * remaining))
                _register_candidate(state, q, compute_exact=True)

    # Refine best surrogate to a larger final length.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    final_p = state.get("sur_p", None)
    if final_p is None:
        final_p = state.get("best_p", None)
    if final_p is None:
        final_p = base

    if remaining > 90.0 and final_p.size < 2048:
        q = _resize_profile(final_p, 2048)
        q, _ = _fourier_schedule(q, time_budget=min(30.0, 0.40 * remaining))
        _register_candidate(state, q, compute_exact=True)
        q, _ = _subgradient_polish(q, time_budget=min(10.0, 0.20 * remaining))
        _register_candidate(state, q, compute_exact=True)
        final_p = q

    # Symmetrization candidate.
    if final_p is not None:
        qs = _normalize(0.5 * final_p + 0.5 * final_p[::-1])
        _register_candidate(state, qs, compute_exact=True)
        s_qs = _hard_score_fft(qs)
        s_fp = _hard_score_fft(final_p)
        if np.isfinite(s_qs) and (not np.isfinite(s_fp) or s_qs < s_fp):
            final_p = qs

    # Long local/block search.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 30.0:
        current = state.get("sur_p", None)
        if current is None:
            current = state.get("best_p", None)
        if current is None:
            current = final_p
        current, _ = _block_search(current, time_budget=max(10.0, remaining - 12.0),
                                   seed=int(seed) + 991)
        _register_candidate(state, current, compute_exact=True)

    # If lots of time remains, do a few fresh random restarts.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    while remaining > 90.0:
        N = 2048 if budget_s > 1000 else 1024
        starts = _generate_starts(N, rng, n_random=8)
        scored = []
        for p in starts:
            sc = _hard_score_fft(p)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                scored.append((float(sc), p))
        scored.sort()
        picks = []
        if scored:
            picks.append(scored[0][1])
            if len(scored) > 12:
                picks.append(scored[len(scored) // 2][1])
        did_work = False
        for p in picks:
            remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if remaining < 50.0:
                break
            p2, _ = _fourier_schedule(p, time_budget=min(20.0, 0.45 * remaining))
            _register_candidate(state, p2, compute_exact=True)
            p3, _ = _subgradient_polish(p2, time_budget=min(10.0, 0.25 * remaining))
            _register_candidate(state, p3, compute_exact=True)
            did_work = True
            remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if not did_work:
            break

    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 45.0:
        fp = state.get("sur_p", None)
        if fp is None:
            fp = state.get("best_p", None)
        fp2, _ = _fourier_schedule(fp, time_budget=min(25.0, 0.50 * remaining))
        _register_candidate(state, fp2, compute_exact=True)
        fp3, _ = _subgradient_polish(fp2, time_budget=min(10.0, 0.20 * remaining))
        _register_candidate(state, fp3, compute_exact=True)
        fp4, _ = _block_search(fp3, time_budget=max(2.0, remaining - 14.0),
                               seed=int(seed) + 2023)
        _register_candidate(state, fp4, compute_exact=True)

    print("before finalization exact =", state.get("best_exact"),
          "sur =", state.get("best_sur"),
          "time left =", deadline - time.monotonic(), flush=True)

    return _finalize_candidate(state, budget_s=budget_s)
```
